But I haven’t really paid attention to whether I remember it later or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following along when you're reading something, like you feel like you're not really taking it in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t read a whole lot, honestly. If it’s like instructions or something, sometimes I have to look back over it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering off to other things when someone is talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. People will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught part of it. Like my supervisor or my wife, I’ll have to ask them to repeat it.\n\nDoctor: Does this happen to you when you're watching TV or listening to the radio alone, or is it mostly when people are talking directly to you?\n\nPatient: Mostly when someone’s talking right to me. Like my wife or my supervisor will say something, and then a minute later I realize I only got part of it and have to ask again. TV or radio… I don’t really notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind is wandering to other things when you're not able to catch what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, they’ll be talking right at me, and then I realize I only caught like half of it. That’s kind of why I’m here—my wife and supervisor keep saying I’m not listening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is wandering to other things on purpose, or is it just kind of drifting away?\n\nPatient: It’s not on purpose. It just kind of… drifts. They’ll be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught half of it and I’m asking them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you're having trouble catching what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really anxious, I guess. Maybe a little frustrated when I have to ask them to repeat it, but I haven’t really thought about it like stress.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It just kind of… drifts. They’ll be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught half of it and I’m asking them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you're having trouble catching what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really anxious, I guess. Maybe a little frustrated when I have to ask them to repeat it, but I haven’t really thought about it like stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing on tasks or activities that you're doing by yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… sometimes I get sidetracked, but I don’t really pay attention to it when I’m by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess if someone shows me, I usually pick it up eventually… but I haven’t really thought about whether learning itself is a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making decisions on your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually pick something if I have to. I haven’t really noticed it being a big problem.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like my supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then a minute later I realize I only caught part of it. Then I have to ask them to repeat themselves. It’s been causing problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're actually hearing what they're saying but just not paying attention, or do you feel like you're not even taking it in to begin with?\n\nPatient: I think I hear them, like the words are there, but I’m not really… taking it in. My supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only got maybe half of it, so I have to ask them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that once someone repeats what they said, you're able to remember it without trouble?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes, yeah, if they repeat it I get it. But I haven’t really paid attention to whether I remember it later or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following along when you're reading something, like you feel like you're not really taking it in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t read a whole lot, honestly. If it’s like instructions or something, sometimes I have to look back over it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering off to other things when someone is talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. People will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught part of it. Like my supervisor or my wife, I’ll have to ask them to repeat it.\n\nDoctor: Does this happen to you when you're watching TV or listening to the radio alone, or is it mostly when people are talking directly to you?\n\nPatient: Mostly when someone’s talking right to me. Like my wife or my supervisor will say something, and then a minute later I realize I only got part of it and have to ask again. TV or radio… I don’t really notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind is wandering to other things when you're not able to catch what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, they’ll be talking right at me, and then I realize I only caught like half of it. That’s kind of why I’m here—my wife and supervisor keep saying I’m not listening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is wandering to other things on purpose, or is it just kind of drifting away?\n\nPatient: It’s not on purpose. It just kind of… drifts. They’ll be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught half of it and I’m asking them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you're having trouble catching what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really anxious, I guess. Maybe a little frustrated when I have to ask them to repeat it, but I haven’t really thought about it like stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing on tasks or activities that you're doing by yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… sometimes I get sidetracked, but I don’t really pay attention to it when I’m by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess if someone shows me, I usually pick it up eventually… but I haven’t really thought about whether learning itself is a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making decisions on your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually pick something if I have to. I haven’t really noticed it being a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened a few days ago?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t usually think back on things from a few days ago unless somebody brings it up.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble catching what people are saying to you?\n\nPatient: Probably a little over two years… about two years and a couple months. It got worse after my wife started working outside the home, and I had to keep up with more of the schedule myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with memory for things that happened recently, aside from not catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. If it’s something recent, I usually remember enough, unless I wasn’t paying much attention in the first place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

My supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only got maybe half of it, so I have to ask them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that once someone repeats what they said, you're able to remember it without trouble?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes, yeah, if they repeat it I get it. But I haven’t really paid attention to whether I remember it later or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following along when you're reading something, like you feel like you're not really taking it in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t read a whole lot, honestly. If it’s like instructions or something, sometimes I have to look back over it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering off to other things when someone is talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I haven’t really noticed it being a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened a few days ago?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. My supervisor or my wife will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only got maybe half of it, so I have to ask them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that once someone repeats what they said, you're able to remember it without trouble?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes, yeah, if they repeat it I get it. But I haven’t really paid attention to whether I remember it later or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following along when you're reading something, like you feel like you're not really taking it in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t read a whole lot, honestly. If it’s like instructions or something, sometimes I have to look back over it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering off to other things when someone is talking to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. People will be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught part of it. Like my supervisor or my wife, I’ll have to ask them to repeat it.\n\nDoctor: Does this happen to you when you're watching TV or listening to the radio alone, or is it mostly when people are talking directly to you?\n\nPatient: Mostly when someone’s talking right to me. Like my wife or my supervisor will say something, and then a minute later I realize I only got part of it and have to ask again. TV or radio… I don’t really notice it as much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind is wandering to other things when you're not able to catch what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, they’ll be talking right at me, and then I realize I only caught like half of it. That’s kind of why I’m here—my wife and supervisor keep saying I’m not listening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your mind is wandering to other things on purpose, or is it just kind of drifting away?\n\nPatient: It’s not on purpose. It just kind of… drifts. They’ll be talking right to me, and then I realize I only caught half of it and I’m asking them to say it again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you're having trouble catching what someone is saying?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really anxious, I guess. Maybe a little frustrated when I have to ask them to repeat it, but I haven’t really thought about it like stress.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing on tasks or activities that you're doing by yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… sometimes I get sidetracked, but I don’t really pay attention to it when I’m by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess if someone shows me, I usually pick it up eventually… but I haven’t really thought about whether learning itself is a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making decisions on your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I can usually pick something if I have to. I haven’t really noticed it being a big problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened a few days ago?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t usually think back on things from a few days ago unless somebody brings it up.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble catching what people are saying to you?\n\nPatient: Probably a little over two years… about two years and a couple months. It got worse after my wife started working outside the home, and I had to keep up with more of the schedule myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty with memory for things that happened recently, aside from not catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. If it’s something recent, I usually remember enough, unless I wasn’t paying much attention in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with your daily routines or responsibilities because of this difficulty catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been causing problems. At work I’ve gotten written up over it, and that’s… not good. I worry I’m going to miss something important on the line, or even lose the job.\n\nAt home too, my wife and the kids get pretty frustrated. I’m supposed to help keep things moving, but sometimes I end up throwing the routine off instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with your wife or kids because of your trouble catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly at home. My wife gets irritated because I miss what she’s telling me and then the whole routine gets messed up. The kids get frustrated too. I’m trying to help, but sometimes I just make things harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding conversations or social situations because of your trouble catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean… I don’t always avoid people, but if I know someone’s going to be explaining something to me, I kind of dread it. People keep saying I’m not listening, and then I realize I only caught half of what they said and have to ask again. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often. If I’m sitting in a safety huddle or even just at dinner, my leg’s usually bouncing, or I’m messing with a pen or my keys. People have told me I’m shaking the whole bench sometimes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m trying to help, but sometimes I just make things harder.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding conversations or social situations because of your trouble catching what people are saying?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah.
